[0m$ [0msed -n '285,315p' .skilled/agents/deep-research.md; echo '---claude'; sed -n '270,295p' .claude/agents/deep-research.md; echo '---pi'; sed -n '278,303p' .pi/agents/deep-research.md
**Append discipline**:
- Record exactly one canonical iteration record through the gateway per iteration.
- Optional idea capture records additional `idea_observed` events through the same gateway when dispatch explicitly allows it.
- Never record `idea_promoted` or `idea_rejected`; reducer and operator workflows own those events.
- Never edit, truncate, sort, or reformat the `research/deep-research-state.jsonl` projection — the gateway owns it.
- If the gateway returns exit 2, or the refreshed projection does not show exactly one new iteration record, return `Status: error` and name the mismatch.

### Step 9: Write Delta

Create `research/deltas/iter-NNN.jsonl` once, as the multi-line delta artifact the reducer consumes.
It is separate from the one-record event file handed to the gateway in Step 8.
Its first line MUST be the canonical iteration record built in Step 8.
Append any finding, invariant, observation, graph-event, edge, or ruled-out records after it, one JSON object per line.
Never overwrite an existing delta file.

**Frontier proposals (expansive depth runs only).** When the iteration prompt carries a FRONTIER block, you may append `frontier_proposal` rows to the delta, at most the number that block allows, one per follow-up question the evidence opened inside the node you were working:
`{"type":"frontier_proposal","parentId":"<the node id the prompt named>","question":"<one new question, not a restatement of an existing one>","scope":"<path, module or area>","evidence":"<file:line or URL that opened it>","budget":<optional positive integer, never above the parent's>}`
You only propose. The workflow's admission step decides, and it checks relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle in that order, so it prunes a row that lacks a question, evidence or scope, names a parent the store does not hold, repeats a known question, would exceed the depth, fan-out or budget caps, or would close a parent cycle. Never write `research/inbox.jsonl` or `research/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, and never emit a `frontier_proposal` row when the prompt has no FRONTIER block.

**newInfoRatio calculation**:
- Fully new findings count as 1.0.
- Partially new findings count as 0.5.
- `newInfoRatio = (fully_new + 0.5 * partially_new) / total_findings`.
- If there are no findings, set 0.0.
- Add a +0.10 simplicity bonus, capped at 1.0, when synthesis reduces open questions, resolves contradictions, or provides a cleaner model without new external evidence.

### Step 10: Update Research (Progressive)

Read `research/deep-research-config.json` before touching `research/research.md`.
- If `progressiveSynthesis == true`, add new findings to `research/research.md`; create it only if missing and only inside the resolved packet root.
- If `progressiveSynthesis == false`, do not create or update `research/research.md`; synthesis owns it later.

[0m$ [0msed -n '250,280p' .skilled/agents/deep-review.md; echo '---claude'; sed -n '240,265p' .claude/agents/deep-review.md; echo '---pi'; sed -n '247,272p' .pi/agents/deep-review.md

Here `<record file>` is a file holding the single canonical iteration record (one JSON object).
The gateway `JSON.parse`s it whole, so it must not be the multi-line `deltas/iter-NNN.jsonl`.
The gateway authorizes the record against the mode's durable authority, fences it behind the ledger, returns a receipt, and refreshes `review/deep-review-state.jsonl` from the ledger.
Exit `0` means the record is durable; exit `2` means it was refused — halt and name the check the refusal reports.
Never fall back to a direct write of the projection file.

#### Step 10: Write Delta

- Create `review/deltas/iter-NNN.jsonl` once — the multi-line delta artifact the reducer consumes, separate from the one-record event file handed to the gateway in Step 9.
- Its first line MUST contain the canonical iteration record built in Step 9.
- Append structured finding, classification, traceability-check, graph-event, and ruled-out records after it, one JSON object per line.
- Never overwrite an existing delta file.

**Frontier proposals (expansive depth runs only).** When the iteration prompt carries a FRONTIER block, you may append `frontier_proposal` rows to the delta, at most the number that block allows, one per sub-target the evidence opened inside the node you were reviewing:
`{"type":"frontier_proposal","parentId":"<the node id the prompt named>","question":"<what to review, not a restatement of an existing node>","scope":"<paths inside the review target>","evidence":"<file:line that opened it>","budget":<optional positive integer, never above the parent's>}`
You only propose. The workflow's admission step decides, and it checks relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle in that order, so it prunes a row that lacks a question, evidence or scope, names a parent the store does not hold, sits outside a `files` review target, repeats a known node, would exceed the depth, fan-out or budget caps, or would close a parent cycle. Never write `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, `searchDebt` or any coverage surface, and never emit a `frontier_proposal` row when the prompt has no FRONTIER block. When the block names a node, add `"frontierNodeId"` to the iteration record as the block says.

#### Step 11: Verify Outputs

- Verify the narrative contains focus, finding severity sections, traceability checks, edge cases, ruled-out section, and next focus.
- Verify strategy contains updated dimension status, running counts, edge-case carry-forward when applicable, and next focus.
- Verify the append gateway returned exit 0 with a receipt, and the refreshed `review/deep-review-state.jsonl` projection shows exactly one new canonical iteration record with complete route proof.
- Verify `review/deltas/iter-NNN.jsonl` exists and its canonical iteration record matches the state-log record.
- Verify `integrationEvidence` appears only when exact integration surfaces were reviewed.
- Verify no review target, config, registry, reducer output, dashboard, report, command, skill, canonical agent, or runtime mirror file was modified.
- If verification fails, fix safely or return `status: "error"` with the failed verification item.

- Never overwrite an existing delta file.

**Frontier proposals (expansive depth runs only).** When the iteration prompt carries a FRONTIER block, you may append `frontier_proposal` rows to the delta, at most the number that block allows, one per sub-target the evidence opened inside the node you were reviewing:
`{"type":"frontier_proposal","parentId":"<the node id the prompt named>","question":"<what to review, not a restatement of an existing node>","scope":"<paths inside the review target>","evidence":"<file:line that opened it>","budget":<optional positive integer, never above the parent's>}`
You only propose. The workflow's admission step decides, and it checks relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle in that order, so it prunes a row that lacks a question, evidence or scope, names a parent the store does not hold, sits outside a `files` review target, repeats a known node, would exceed the depth, fan-out or budget caps, or would close a parent cycle. Never write `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, `searchDebt` or any coverage surface, and never emit a `frontier_proposal` row when the prompt has no FRONTIER block. When the block names a node, add `"frontierNodeId"` to the iteration record as the block says.

#### Step 11: Verify Outputs

- Verify the narrative contains focus, finding severity sections, traceability checks, edge cases, ruled-out section, and next focus.
- Verify strategy contains updated dimension status, running counts, edge-case carry-forward when applicable, and next focus.
- Verify the append gateway returned exit 0 with a receipt, and the refreshed `review/deep-review-state.jsonl` projection shows exactly one new canonical iteration record with complete route proof.
- Verify `review/deltas/iter-NNN.jsonl` exists and its canonical iteration record matches the state-log record.
- Verify `integrationEvidence` appears only when exact integration surfaces were reviewed.
- Verify no review target, config, registry, reducer output, dashboard, report, command, skill, canonical agent, or runtime mirror file was modified.
- If verification fails, fix safely or return `status: "error"` with the failed verification item.

### Workflow Flag Surface

The argument hint names the invocation shape; the whole flag surface is here. Every flag below
is a workflow input, never an execution mode, and the presentation asset owns how each one binds
into `config`.

| Flag | Value | Effect |
|------|-------|--------|
| `--spec-folder` | `PATH` | Binds the packet that owns this run's writes. |
| `--max-iterations` | `N` | Hard ceiling on loop iterations. |
| `--convergence` | `N` | Threshold the loop must clear before convergence may stop it. |
| `--convergence-mode` | `default` \| `off` \| `sliding-window` \| `divergent` | `default` keeps the anti-convergence `minIterations` floor. `off` disables convergence-driven STOP while max-iterations, pause and halt stay active. `sliding-window` scores novelty over a bounded window, five snapshots by default. `divergent` routes an eligible convergence stop into a Council pivot rather than ending the run. |
| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1 (merges are pruned as `merge-no-own-diff` and index rebuilds as `generated-artifact`), admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths, and a run-wide `maxFrontierNodes` cap that defaults to half of `--max-iterations`), keeps node status in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, holds open nodes as `searchDebt`, dispatches the active node's next uncovered dimension, turns a review stop candidate into another iteration while nodes stay open, and, once every node is closed and at least one was answered, forms a `frontier_drained` stop candidate that the legal-stop gates still decide, so the run can stop before its cap. A node needs one iteration per dimension in its universe, the four canonical dimensions plus any custom name, and the depth-1 band gets only `budgetSplit.depth1` (0.5) of `--max-iterations`, so set `--max-iterations` to at least `2 * ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions` to fund the depth-1 band. It never outranks the iteration cap, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. Each node needs at least one iteration, so growth past depth 1 needs `maxIterations` above twice the live node count. At the default budget of 7 an expansive run works its seeds only; raise `--max-iterations`, as the pilots did at 16, to reach depths 2 and 3. Fan-out lineages each get a share of the seeded nodes. |
| `--lineage-timeout-hours` | `N` | See Lineage Timeout Flag below. |
| `--stop-policy` | `convergence` \| `max-iterations` | See Stop Policy Flag below. |
| `--no-resource-map` | no value | Suppresses the resource-map write; the review report still emits. |
| `--dry-run` | no value | See Dry-Run Flag below. |
| `--review-focus` | `"TEXT"` | Optional operator focus that every iteration covers in addition to its dimension, in single-executor and fan-out runs alike. It is cut to one line of at most 1000 characters, stored as `reviewFocus` in the config and shown in the review report. |
| `--restart`, `--lineage-mode` | `auto` \| `resume` \| `restart` | Session lifecycle intent, default `auto`. `resume` keeps the same `sessionId` and archives nothing; `restart`, which bare `--restart` selects, opens a new `sessionId` at generation+1 and archives the prior `review/`. `fork` is not accepted. |
| `--extend` | positive integer, as `--extend=N` | Extends a completed run in place by `N` iterations, lineage mode `completed-continue`. The run keeps its `sessionId` and generation, records one `run_extended` ledger event and keeps the prior review report as `review/review-report-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md`. The run must be complete, so use `--lineage-mode=resume` for an unfinished one. Refused with fan-out. Under `stopPolicy: convergence` an extension with nothing new can re-stop on `all_dimensions_clean` at once: pass `--convergence-mode=off` or use `stopPolicy: max-iterations`. |
| `--extend-minutes` | positive integer, as `--extend-minutes=M` | Minutes added to the duration budget on an extension, default the prior `maxDurationMinutes`. The new budget is `max(prior, elapsed) + M`, because the clock counts idle time since `createdAt`, so an extension started after the old budget still runs. Valid only with `--extend`. |
| `--executor` | `TYPE` | Selects the executor kind, and is repeatable. Each occurrence opens a group accepting `--model=X`, `--config-dir=PATH`, `--reasoning-effort=LEVEL`, `--service-tier=TIER`, `--executor-timeout=SECONDS`, `--iters=N`, `--count=N` and `--label=X`. Which of those a given kind supports is in the presentation asset. |
| `--executors` | `JSON` | Declares the executor matrix in one value; one expanded lineage selects `config.executor`, while two or more select `config.fanout`. |
| `--concurrency` | `N` | Caps concurrent lineages in the fan-out pool. Default `2`. |

Fan-out policy: zero or one `--executor` runs the single-executor path, and so does an
`--executors` matrix that expands to exactly one lineage. Its `iterations` value binds to
`config.maxIterations`, and its executor settings populate `config.executor`. Two or more
`--executor` flags, a matrix expanding to two or more lineages, or any `--count` above 1
selects fan-out. A direct call of the fan-out runner that expands to one research or review
lineage is refused and names this workflow instead, because the runner alone skips packet
init, synthesis and save. `--fanout-lineage-artifact-dir` is internal and
set by the runtime, not by a caller. Under `:auto` a `PRE-BOUND SETUP ANSWERS:` block in the
---
  const raw = args[key];
  if (raw === undefined || raw === false || raw === null || raw === '') {
    return null;
  }
  if (raw === true || typeof raw !== 'string') {
    throw inputError(`${key} must be a number`);
  }
  const value = Number(raw);
  if (!Number.isFinite(value) || value < 0) {
    throw inputError(`${key} must be a non-negative number`);
  }
  return value;
}

const REVIEW_FOCUS_MAX_LENGTH = 1000;
// C0 and C1 controls, DEL, and the Unicode line and paragraph separators. Each would
// split a one-line setup binding or the native command's argument into a second line.
const REVIEW_FOCUS_CONTROL_PATTERN = /[\u0000-\u001F\u007F-\u009F\u2028\u2029]/g;

/**
 * Normalize the operator's optional review focus into one bounded line.
 *
 * The focus travels as a single `review_focus:` setup binding in every lineage prompt, so a
 * control character would end that binding early and let the rest read as new instructions.
 *
 * @param {unknown} raw - The `--review-focus` value as parsed from argv.
 * @returns {string|null} The single-line focus text, or null when no focus was given.
 */
function normalizeReviewFocus(raw) {
  if (raw === undefined || raw === null || raw === false || raw === '') {
    return null;
  }
  if (typeof raw !== 'string') {
    throw inputError('reviewFocus must be a text value');
  }
  const focus = raw.replace(REVIEW_FOCUS_CONTROL_PATTERN, ' ').replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim();
  if (focus === '') {
    return null;
  }
  if (focus.length > REVIEW_FOCUS_MAX_LENGTH) {
    throw inputError(`reviewFocus must be at most ${REVIEW_FOCUS_MAX_LENGTH} characters, got ${focus.length}`);
  }
  return focus;
}

 * A review can audit something other than its own packet. Without an explicit target each

The new budget is `max(prior, elapsed) + M`, because the clock counts idle time since `createdAt`, so an extension started after the old budget still runs. Valid only with `--extend`. |

.skilled/skills/mcp-tooling/mcp-refero/manual-testing-playbook/safety-gate/quota-recovery.md:19:The published limit surface is deliberately thin: only the monthly Pro quota exists in any authoritative source, and per-second, burst, concurrency, and authenticated-429 behavior are unknown across the entire research record. .skilled/skills/cli-external-orchestration/cli-opencode/manual-testing-playbook/session-continuity/resume-by-session-id.md:50:| CO-019 | Resume specific session by id (-s) | Confirm `-s <id>` resumes the named session and the resumed turn references prior content | `Run one opencode turn, capture its session id, resume with -s, and verify the follow-up references prior content.` | 1. `bash: opencode run --model llmgateway/deepseek-v4.1-flash-1017 --variant max --format json --dir "$(pwd)" "Define a fictional concept named the (echo-cascade verifier): it is a CRDT replication primitive that uses cascaded echoes to detect lost updates. State that the cascade depth is fixed at 7." > /tmp/co-019-turn1.jsonl 2>&1; echo "TURN1: $?"; SID=$(jq -r 'select(.type == "step_start") \| .sessionID' /tmp/co-019-turn1.jsonl \| head -1); echo "Captured sessionID: $SID"; opencode run --model llmgateway/deepseek-v4.1-flash-1017 --variant max --format json --dir "$(pwd)" -s "$SID" "What is the cascade depth we discussed? Answer in one short sentence." > /tmp/co-019-turn2.jsonl 2>&1; echo "TURN2: $?"; jq -r 'select(.type == "text") \| .part.text' /tmp/co-019-turn2.jsonl \| grep -ciE '(echo-cascade\|cascade depth\|7\|seven)'; jq -r 'select(.type == "step_start") \| .sessionID' /tmp/co-019-turn2.jsonl \| head -1` (OpenCode 1 only, with the OpenCode 2 twin as `cd "$(pwd)" && opencode run --standalone -m llmgateway/deepseek-v4.1-flash-1017#max --format json "<prompt>"` for turn 1 and the same with `-s "$SID"` for the resume, once `opencode models --standalone` lists the `llmgateway/` prefix of the model you pass to `-m`) | Step 1 (single shell): TURN1 exit 0 with a `step_start` event present. SID captured non-empty. TURN2 exit 0 using that SAME `$SID`. Count of cascade/depth/7/seven references >= 1. TURN2 `sessionID` matches the captured SID (resume worked, not a new session) | `/tmp/co-019-turn1.jsonl`, `/tmp/co-019-turn2.jsonl`, captured SID in terminal | PASS if both exit 0 AND SID captured AND second turn references prior content AND second turn session id matches captured SID. FAIL if SID capture fails, second turn cannot resume, or second turn opens a fresh session | 1. If `jq` returns empty for `sessionID`, the JSON event stream may use a different field name. Inspect the raw line. 2. If `-s <id>` is rejected with "session not found", the session may have expired or the id may be malformed. Re-capture from a fresh dispatch. 3. If second turn opens a new session id, the resume failed silently. Verify the captured id via `opencode export "$SID"` rather than a state-directory read. 4. If `SID` is empty in the second half of the command, the launch and capture steps were accidentally split across separate tool-call shells. Re-run as one shell block. 5. If the prior content is not referenced, the resume may have loaded the wrong session. Try `--continue` instead and capture which session it resumed |

.skilled/skills/sk-doc/sk-create-skill/references/shared/validation-and-packaging.md:136:- **Render checks (script)**: The deterministic half of content quality. `validate_document.py --json` reports `render_*` warnings for a compound overview heading, an overview that opens without orientation, a joined bold label, a setext underline, a bare URL, a repeated sentence or label, a letter-suffixed H2 number, a related section that is not last and an intro that restates the Purpose. .skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/feature-catalog/tooling-and-scripts/spec-folder-literal-naming-create-sh-fallback.md:20:This is the script-level half of the literal-naming discipline. When an operator runs `create.sh "task" --phase --phases N` without `--phase-names`, every generated child folder ends with the literal token `PROVIDE-DESCRIPTIVE-SLUG` and the script emits one `[speckit] Warning:` line per child to stderr. .skilled/skills/sk-code/sk-code-quality/manual-testing-playbook/quality-gate/rule-override-conflict.md:28:A conflict is the one case the precedence table cannot settle, and the runner's answer is to keep the default and make the rule an open claim rather than to pick a side. That is a deliberate choice with two failure directions. A runner that picked the first checklist would silently apply half of a rule set its author never agreed to; a runner that applied both would leave a contradiction for the agent to resolve later. The third failure is quieter: a row that conflicts must not keep serving files in its `files` list, because a caller reading `rule_overrides` alone would see a row that applies to nothing.

This is the hook's original check; the dist-staleness branch validated by TH-001 was added later and runs alongside it on the same edit. When run directly, the script calls `.skilled/skills/sk-code/sk-code-quality/scripts/check-comment-hygiene.sh` against the file it is given. If a comment line carries an ephemeral-artifact pointer (a spec path, packet/phase number, or ADR/REQ/task/finding id, the forbidden classes in `sk-code-quality/references/code-style-guide.md` §4), the checker prints each offending line to stdout as `<file>:<lineno>: <excerpt>` and exits 1. When the hook sees that `returncode == 1` with non-empty stdout, it wraps those lines in a `COMMENT HYGIENE WARNING:` banner. The hook is warn-only: it always exits 0 and never blocks the edit, regardless of what the checker finds or whether the checker itself fails.
.skilled/skills/sk-code/manual-testing-playbook/tooling-and-hooks/comment-hygiene-hook.md:153:The comment-hygiene hook is an **author-side** gate: it runs at edit time inside `sk-code-quality` (the `sk-code` quality workflow mode) and its warn-only banner nudges the author to replace an ephemeral-artifact pointer with a durable WHY before the code ever reaches a reviewer. .skilled/commands/deep/review.md:100:| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1 (merges are pruned as `merge-no-own-diff` and index rebuilds as `generated-artifact`), admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths, and a run-wide `maxFrontierNodes` cap that defaults to half of `--max-iterations`), keeps node status in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, holds open nodes as `searchDebt`, dispatches the active node's next uncovered dimension, turns a review stop candidate into another iteration while nodes stay open, and, once every node is closed and at least one was answered, forms a `frontier_drained` stop candidate that the legal-stop gates still decide, so the run can stop before its cap. A node needs one iteration per dimension in its universe, the four canonical dimensions plus any custom name, and the depth-1 band gets only `budgetSplit.depth1` (0.5) of `--max-iterations`, so set `--max-iterations` to at least `2 * ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions` to fund the depth-1 band. It never outranks the iteration cap, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. Each node needs at least one iteration, so growth past depth 1 needs `maxIterations` above twice the live node count. At the default budget of 7 an expansive run works its seeds only; raise `--max-iterations`, as the pilots did at 16, to reach depths 2 and 3. Fan-out lineages each get a share of the seeded nodes. |

.skilled/commands/deep/review.md:100:| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1 (merges are pruned as `merge-no-own-diff` and index rebuilds as `generated-artifact`), admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths, and a run-wide `maxFrontierNodes` cap that defaults to half of `--max-iterations`), keeps node status in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, holds open nodes as `searchDebt`, dispatches the active node's next uncovered dimension, turns a review stop candidate into another iteration while nodes stay open, and, once every node is closed and at least one was answered, forms a `frontier_drained` stop candidate that the legal-stop gates still decide, so the run can stop before its cap. A node needs one iteration per dimension in its universe, the four canonical dimensions plus any custom name, and the depth-1 band gets only `budgetSplit.depth1` (0.5) of `--max-iterations`, so set `--max-iterations` to at least `2 * ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions` to fund the depth-1 band. It never outranks the iteration cap, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. Each node needs at least one iteration, so growth past depth 1 needs `maxIterations` above twice the live node count. At the default budget of 7 an expansive run works its seeds only; raise `--max-iterations`, as the pilots did at 16, to reach depths 2 and 3. Fan-out lineages each get a share of the seeded nodes. |

It never outranks the iteration cap, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. Each node needs at least one iteration, so growth past depth 1 needs `maxIterations` above twice the live node count. At the default budget of 10 an expansive run works its seeds only; raise `--max-iterations`, as the pilots did at 16, to reach depths 2 and 3. Fan-out lineages each get a share of the seeded nodes. |

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/feature-catalog/deep-loop-mode-classification/depth-mode-flag.md:16:`--depth-mode` is a command-level option on `/deep:research` and `/deep:review`. It takes `default` or `expansive`. The flag is not part of `mode-registry.json`, so the hub classifies a request into the same workflow mode with or without it. The behavior the flag turns on lives in the research and review packets. Admission caps the live frontier at half of `maxIterations` by default, and the seed counts toward that cap, so at the default budgets an expansive run works its seeds only; raise `--max-iterations`, as the pilots did at 16, to reach depths 2 and 3.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/SKILL.md:238:- The flag writes `antiConvergence.depthMode` and the caps in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6 (the review root seed takes up to 8 depth-1 sub-targets, against 6 for research), a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations` unless the config sets a number. The depth-1 seed answers only to the root-children cap, but every seed that is not pruned counts toward the run cap. Each node needs at least one iteration, so growth past depth 1 needs `maxIterations` above twice the live node count. At the default budget of 7 an expansive run works its seeds only; raise `--max-iterations`, as the pilots did at 16, to reach depths 2 and 3. `antiConvergence.depthMode` is locked against the optimizer; the `expansiveDepth` caps are in neither its tunable nor its locked list.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/convergence/convergence.md:105:`--depth-mode=expansive` grows a depth-capped frontier of sub-target nodes beside the review target. The value lands in `antiConvergence.depthMode`, and the caps live in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `budgetSplit` of 0.5/0.35/0.15 across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations` unless the config sets a number. The run cap never prunes a depth-1 seed, which only the root-children cap bounds, but every seed that is not pruned, answered ones included, counts toward the live total that later admissions are checked against. Each node needs at least one iteration, so growth past depth 1 needs `maxIterations` above twice the live node count. At the default budget of 7 an expansive run works its seeds only; raise `--max-iterations`, as the pilots did at 16, to reach depths 2 and 3. A fan-out parent seeds before any config exists, so its cap falls back to 10. The mode is locked against the optimizer; the `expansiveDepth` block is in neither its tunable nor its locked list. An absent flag means `default`, which runs exactly as before.

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md:158:`--depth-mode=expansive` is the one stop-path change that grows work instead of ending it. A seed step writes the depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, the active node's next uncovered dimension steers the iteration, and an admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, pruning the proposal on the first failure. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, which no default run creates, and an open node is filed as open search debt, so the run spends the iteration that node still needs. The frontier veto never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the floor above, and stuck recovery closes the active node as `exhausted`. Once every node is closed and at least one was answered, a run that forms no other stop candidate gets `frontier_drained`, which the legal-stop gates still decide, so a run whose frontier work is done stops before its cap. The caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations`. Each node needs at least one iteration, so growth past depth 1 needs `maxIterations` above twice the live node count. At the default budget of 7 an expansive run works its seeds only; raise `--max-iterations`, as the pilots did at 16, to reach depths 2 and 3.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/expansive-depth-mode.md:28:The active node's next uncovered dimension is dispatched. A node needs one iteration per dimension in its universe, the four canonical dimensions plus any custom name. Each depth band starts from its `budgetSplit` share of `--max-iterations` (0.5, 0.35 and 0.15), plus any share an earlier band left unspent once that band holds no open node, so fund the depth-1 band with at least `2 * ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions`. The run-wide `maxFrontierNodes` cap defaults to half of `maxIterations`, and every seed that is not pruned counts toward it. Each node needs at least one iteration, so growth past depth 1 needs `maxIterations` above twice the live node count. At the default budget of 7 an expansive run works its seeds only; raise `--max-iterations`, as the pilots did at 16, to reach depths 2 and 3.

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/README.md:127:`--depth-mode=expansive` lets a run grow its own questions. A seed step writes 3 to 6 depth-1 nodes before iteration 1, and the leaf may propose children only when its prompt carries a frontier block. An admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, and prunes the proposal on the first failure. Next Focus reads the shallowest open node first and writes it as a frontier line, so visiting a node does not read as answering it. While a node stays open the frontier vetoes a soft convergence stop, which never outranks the iteration cap or the other hard stops, and the caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations`. Each node needs at least one iteration, so growth past depth 1 needs `maxIterations` above twice the live node count. At the default budget of 10 an expansive run works its seeds only; raise `--max-iterations`, as the pilots did at 16, to reach depths 2 and 3.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/convergence/convergence.md:68:`--depth-mode=expansive` grows a frontier of follow-up questions beside the questions seeded at init. The value lands in `antiConvergence.depthMode`, and the caps live in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, `budgetSplit` 0.5/0.35/0.15 for depths 1, 2 and 3, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations` unless the config sets a number. The run cap never prunes a depth-1 seed, which only the root-children cap bounds, but every seed that is not pruned, answered ones included, counts toward the live total that later admissions are checked against. Each node needs at least one iteration, so growth past depth 1 needs `maxIterations` above twice the live node count. At the default budget of 10 an expansive run works its seeds only; raise `--max-iterations`, as the pilots did at 16, to reach depths 2 and 3. A fan-out parent seeds before any config exists, so its cap falls back to 10. An absent flag means `default`, which keeps the default behavior; `research-depth-mode-golden.vitest` proves it after stripping the expansive-only persona paragraph from the CLI lineage prompt, the one byte change.

Each node needs at least one iteration, so growth past depth 1 needs `maxIterations` above twice the live node count. At the default budget of 10 an expansive run works its seeds only; raise `--max-iterations`, as the pilots did at 16, to reach depths 2 and 3. A fan-out parent seeds before any config exists, so its cap falls back to 10. An absent flag means `default`, which keeps the default behavior; `research-depth-mode-golden.vitest` proves it after stripping the expansive-only persona paragraph from the CLI lineage prompt, the one byte change.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/README.md:127:`--depth-mode=expansive` lets a run grow its own questions. A seed step writes 3 to 6 depth-1 nodes before iteration 1, and the leaf may propose children only when its prompt carries a frontier block. An admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, and prunes the proposal on the first failure. Next Focus reads the shallowest open node first and writes it as a frontier line, so visiting a node does not read as answering it. .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/expansive-depth-mode.md:28:The active node's next uncovered dimension is dispatched. .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md:158:`--depth-mode=expansive` is the one stop-path change that grows work instead of ending it. A seed step writes the depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, the active node's next uncovered dimension steers the iteration, and an admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, pruning the proposal on the first failure. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, which no default run creates, and an open node is filed as open search debt, so the run spends the iteration that node still needs. The frontier veto never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the floor above, and stuck recovery closes the active node as `exhausted`. Once every node is closed and at least one was answered, a run that forms no other stop candidate gets `frontier_drained`, which the legal-stop gates still decide, so a run whose frontier work is done stops before its cap. The caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations`. Each node needs at least one iteration, so growth past depth 1 needs `maxIterations` above twice the live node count. At the default budget of 7 an expansive run works its seeds only; raise `--max-iterations`, as the pilots did at 16, to reach depths 2 and 3.

# DRV-082 -- Review fan-out deals seeded frontier nodes round-robin

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DRV-082`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates how a review fan-out run splits its depth-one nodes across lineages and checks the split at merge time for `DRV-082`. It focuses on the round-robin deal in node-id order, on each lineage's node store, which is written before the lineage spawns and carries no inbox, and on the merge guard that names a dealt node no lineage reports.

### Why This Matters

A fan-out review that drops a sub-target without saying so looks complete. The partition makes each node belong to one lineage, and the guard makes a lost node visible at merge time instead of letting the merged frontier look finished.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run the commands below and confirm that the deal is round-robin, that each lineage store is written before spawn, that a default run deals nothing and that the merge guard names a missing node.

- Objective: Confirm that depth-one nodes are dealt round-robin in node-id order across lineages, that each lineage store is written with no inbox, that a default lineage prompt gains no depth-mode bytes and that the merge guard fails and names a dealt node that no lineage reports.
- Real user request: Split the review across lineages and show me that no sub-target is dropped.
- Prompt: `Check that a review fan-out run under expansive depth mode deals its depth-one nodes round-robin across its lineages, writes each lineage store without an inbox and fails the merge guard when a dealt node reaches no lineage. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`
- Expected execution process: Run the review fan-out cases by file stem, run the review merge cases by describe name, then read the partition helper, the store writer and the guard line in the fan-out and merge scripts.
- Expected signals: Seven nodes deal to the three lineages as left `F-1-001`, `F-1-004`, `F-1-007`, center `F-1-002`, `F-1-005` and right `F-1-003`, `F-1-006`. The lineage store lands at the lineage directory root with no `inbox.jsonl` file. The default lineage prompt gains no depth-mode bytes. The merge guard passes when every dealt node is reported and fails with the missing identifier when one is not. The merge output carries `frontier_partition_guard`.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator can say every seeded sub-target reached a lineage, or the merge names the one that did not.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the fan-out cases, the merge cases and the guard line pass or print; FAIL if the deal is not round-robin, a lineage store is missing before spawn, an inbox appears for a review lineage, or a missing node passes the guard.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prerequisites

- Working directory is the repository root.
- The `runtime/` vitest suites are installed under `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime`.

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Check that a review fan-out run under expansive depth mode deals its depth-one nodes round-robin across its lineages, writes each lineage store without an inbox and fails the merge guard when a dealt node reaches no lineage. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`

### Commands

1. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run fanout-run.vitest -t "deals seven review frontier nodes|writes a review lineage store without an inbox|binds a review frontier scope|adds no depth-mode bytes to a default review lineage prompt" --reporter=verbose`
2. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run fanout-merge.vitest -t "review frontier merge" --reporter=verbose`
3. `rg -n "partitionFrontierNodes|writeLineageFrontierStore|frontier_scope" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-run.cjs`
4. `rg -n -F "frontier_partition_guard_failed" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs`

### Expected

Command 1 prints `Tests  4 passed` (the rest of the file skipped). The round-robin case asserts the deal above, the store case asserts a `frontier-nodes.jsonl` file at the lineage directory root with no `inbox.jsonl`, the scope case asserts the `frontier_scope` binding beside `review_focus`, and the default case asserts no depth-mode bytes in the lineage prompt. Command 2 prints `Tests  5 passed` (the rest of the file skipped). Its cases pass the guard when every dealt node is reported, fail it with `missing: ['F-1-004']` when one is not, merge each lineage frontier with its label, give every merged node a lineage-qualified id when lineages number their children alike and add no frontier key when no review lineage has one. Command 3 prints the partition helper, the store writer, the `frontier_scope` prompt bindings and the store call, which passes `inbox: false` for a review lineage. Command 4 prints one line, which writes the `frontier_partition_guard_failed` warning with the missing identifiers.

### Evidence

- The verbose case lines and summary lines from commands 1 and 2, with each exit status.
- The `rg` lines from commands 3 and 4, read from the current scripts.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: Commands 1 and 2 exit 0 with the expected counts, and commands 3 and 4 print their lines.
- **Fail**: The deal is not round-robin in node-id order, a review lineage gets an inbox, a missing node passes the guard, or command 4 prints nothing.

### Failure Triage

1. The deal differs from the round-robin order: check the partition helper, which must take the open or active depth-one nodes, sort them by node id and then deal them across the labels in turn.
2. A lineage store lands in the wrong place or appears with an inbox: the lineage store belongs at `lineages/<label>/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, and a review lineage must not receive an inbox.
3. The merge guard stays silent on a missing node: check that the guard reads the recorded partition from the orchestration summary, and that the merged output carries `frontier_partition_guard`.

---

# DR-073 -- Fan-out deals seeded frontier nodes round-robin

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DR-073`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates how the expansive frontier crosses a fan-out run. The objective is to confirm that the seeded nodes are dealt across the lineages before any lineage spawns, and that the merge reports a node that no lineage holds.

### Why This Matters

Each lineage works only the nodes it holds. A node that no lineage receives is never worked, and its question stays open for the whole run. The guard makes that loss visible at merge time.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run the exact commands below and confirm the partition, the store write before spawn, the merge guard and the default path.

- Objective: Confirm that the depth-one nodes are dealt round-robin, that each lineage store exists before its lineage spawns, that the merge guard fails on a missing partitioned node and that a default run writes no lineage store.
- Real user request: Split this research across three lineages, and tell me whether every seeded sub-question reached one of them.
- Prompt: `Check that a fan-out research run under expansive depth mode deals the seeded depth-one nodes round-robin across its lineages, writes each lineage store before spawn, and fails the merge guard when a partitioned node is missing. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`
- Expected execution process: Run the partition, store-before-spawn, default-path and merge-guard cases by file stem, then read the partition helpers in the fan-out runner.
- Expected signals: The round-robin case passes. The store-before-spawn case passes. The default-path case passes and shows no lineage store. The merge guard passes when every partitioned node is reported, and fails when one is missing from every lineage. The runner writes `frontier_scope:` only under the expansive flag.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator can say each seeded sub-question went to one lineage before spawn, and a missing one would be named at merge.
- Pass/fail: PASS if all four named cases pass and the runner shows the partition and store helpers; FAIL if a seeded node is dealt to no lineage, a store is written after spawn or a default run writes a store.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prerequisites

- Working directory is the repository root.
- The `runtime/` vitest suites are installed under `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime`.

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Check that a fan-out research run under expansive depth mode deals the seeded depth-one nodes round-robin across its lineages, writes each lineage store before spawn, and fails the merge guard when a partitioned node is missing. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`

### Commands

1. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run fanout-run.vitest -t "round-robin" --reporter=verbose`
2. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run fanout-run.vitest -t "writes the lineage store before spawn" --reporter=verbose`
3. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run fanout-run.vitest -t "default depth mode" --reporter=verbose`
4. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run fanout-merge.vitest -t "partitioned node" --reporter=verbose`
5. `rg -n "frontier_scope|partitionFrontierNodes|writeLineageFrontierStore" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-run.cjs`

### Expected

Command 1 prints `Tests  2 passed` (the rest of the file skipped), with the cases `deals open depth-1 nodes round-robin in node-id order` and `deals seven review frontier nodes over three seats round-robin` passing. Command 2 prints `Tests  1 passed` (the rest of the file skipped), with the case `writes the lineage store before spawn and records the partition` passing. Command 3 prints `Tests  1 passed` (the rest of the file skipped), with the case `a default depth mode writes no lineage store and records no partition` passing. Command 4 prints `Tests  4 passed` (the rest of the file skipped), with the cases `passes the guard when every partitioned node is reported` and `fails the guard when a partitioned node is missing from every lineage` passing under both the research and the review frontier merge. Command 5 prints the partition helper, the store writer and the `frontier_scope` binding.

### Evidence

- The verbose case lines and summary lines from commands 1 to 4, with each exit status.
- The `rg` lines from command 5, read from the current source.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: All four commands exit 0 with the expected counts, and command 5 shows the partition and store helpers.
- **Fail**: A seeded node goes to no lineage, a store is written after spawn, a default run writes a store, or the merge guard passes a missing node.

### Failure Triage

- A seeded node goes to no lineage: check the round-robin deal over node-id order in the partition helper.
- A store appears after spawn: check that the store writer runs in the lineage worker before the prompt is built and spawned.
- The merge guard passes a missing node: check that the guard reads the partition recorded in the orchestration summary.

const lineageA: LineageDataFixture = {
    label: 'a',
    registry: registryWithFrontier([frontierNode('F-1-001', 'open'), frontierNode('F-2-003', 'exhausted')]),
  };
  const lineageB: LineageDataFixture = {
    label: 'b',
    registry: registryWithFrontier([frontierNode('F-1-002', 'answered'), frontierNode('F-2-003', 'open')]),
  };

  const withoutFrontier = ({ label, registry }: LineageDataFixture): LineageDataFixture => {
    const { frontier: _frontier, ...rest } = registry as Record<string, unknown>;
    return { label, registry: rest };
  };

  it('merges each lineage frontier with its label', () => {
    const frontier = mergeResearchFrontier([lineageA, lineageB]);
    expect(frontier.nodes).toHaveLength(4);
    expect(frontier.nodes.filter((node) => node.lineageNodeId === 'F-2-003').map((node) => node.nodeId)).toEqual(['a:F-2-003', 'b:F-2-003']);
    expect(frontier.stats.total).toBe(4);
    expect(frontier.stats.open).toBe(2);
    expect(frontier.stats.exhausted).toBe(1);
    expect(frontier.stats.answered).toBe(1);
  });

    const frontier = mergeResearchFrontier([lineageA, lineageB]);
    const ids = frontier.nodes.map((node) => node.nodeId);

    expect(new Set(ids).size).toBe(ids.length);
  });

  it('adds no frontier key when no lineage has one', () => {
    const merged = mergeResearchRegistries([withoutFrontier(lineageA), withoutFrontier(lineageB)]);

    });

    expect(frontier.partitionGuard).toEqual({ passed: true, missing: [] });

    const merged = mergeResearchRegistries([lineageA, lineageB], {
      frontierPartition: { a: ['F-1-001', 'F-1-004'], b: ['F-1-002'] },
    });
    const frontier = merged.frontier as { partitionGuard?: { passed: boolean; missing: string[] } };
    expect(frontier.partitionGuard).toEqual({ passed: false, missing: ['F-1-004'] });
  });

describe('review frontier merge', () => {
  const frontierNode = (nodeId: string, status: string) => ({ nodeId, status });

  const registryWithFrontier = (nodes: Array<{ nodeId: string; status: string }>) => ({
    openFindings: [],
    resolvedFindings: [],
    frontier: { nodes, stats: {} },
  });

  const lineageA: LineageDataFixture = {
    label: 'a',
    registry: registryWithFrontier([frontierNode('F-1-001', 'open'), frontierNode('F-2-003', 'exhausted')]),
  };
  const lineageB: LineageDataFixture = {
    label: 'b',
    registry: registryWithFrontier([frontierNode('F-1-002', 'answered'), frontierNode('F-2-003', 'open')]),
  };

  const withoutFrontier = ({ label, registry }: LineageDataFixture): LineageDataFixture => {
    const { frontier: _frontier, ...rest } = registry as Record<string, unknown>;
    return { label, registry: rest };
  };

  it('merges each lineage frontier with its label and stats', () => {
    const merged = mergeReviewRegistries([lineageA, lineageB]);
    const frontier = merged.frontier as {
      nodes: Array<{ nodeId: string; lineageNodeId: string; lineage: string }>;
      stats: Record<string, number>;
    };
    expect(frontier.nodes).toHaveLength(4);
    expect(frontier.nodes.filter((node) => node.lineageNodeId === 'F-2-003').map((node) => node.nodeId)).toEqual(['a:F-2-003', 'b:F-2-003']);
    expect(frontier.stats).toMatchObject({ total: 4, open: 2, exhausted: 1, answered: 1 });
  });

  it('passes the guard when every partitioned node is reported', () => {
    const merged = mergeReviewRegistries([lineageA, lineageB], {
      frontierPartition: { a: ['F-1-001'], b: ['F-1-002'] },
    });
    const frontier = merged.frontier as { partitionGuard?: { passed: boolean; missing: string[] } };
    expect(frontier.partitionGuard).toEqual({ passed: true, missing: [] });
  });

  it('fails the guard when a partitioned node is missing from every lineage', () => {
    const merged = mergeReviewRegistries([lineageA, lineageB], {
      frontierPartition: { a: ['F-1-001', 'F-1-004'], b: ['F-1-002'] },
    });
    const frontier = merged.frontier as { partitionGuard?: { passed: boolean; missing: string[] } };
    expect(frontier.partitionGuard).toEqual({ passed: false, missing: ['F-1-004'] });
  });

  it('gives every merged node a unique id when lineages number their children alike', () => {
    // Each lineage numbers the nodes it admits from its own store, so three lineages that
    // each hold one dealt depth-1 node all name their first child F-2-002.
    const lineage = (label: string, parentId: string) => ({
      label,
      registry: registryWithFrontier([
        { nodeId: parentId, parentId: 'root', status: 'answered' } as { nodeId: string; status: string },
        { nodeId: 'F-2-002', parentId, status: 'pruned' } as { nodeId: string; status: string },
      ]),
    });
    const merged = mergeReviewRegistries([lineage('pi-2', 'F-1-076'), lineage('pi-3', 'F-1-077'), lineage('pi-4', 'F-1-078')], {
      frontierPartition: { 'pi-2': ['F-1-076'], 'pi-3': ['F-1-077'], 'pi-4': ['F-1-078'] },
    });
    const frontier = merged.frontier as {
      nodes: Array<{ nodeId: string; lineageNodeId: string; parentId: string; lineage: string }>;
      partitionGuard?: { passed: boolean; missing: string[] };
    };
    const ids = frontier.nodes.map((node) => node.nodeId);

    expect(new Set(ids).size).toBe(ids.length);
    const child = frontier.nodes.find((node) => node.lineage === 'pi-3' && node.lineageNodeId === 'F-2-002');
    expect(child).toBeDefined();
    expect(ids).toContain(child?.parentId);
    expect(frontier.partitionGuard).toEqual({ passed: true, missing: [] });
  });

  it('adds no frontier key when no review lineage has one', () => {
    const merged = mergeReviewRegistries([withoutFrontier(lineageA), withoutFrontier(lineageB)]);
    expect(Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(merged, 'frontier')).toBe(false);
  });
});

[0m$ [0msed -n '70,95p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/state/state-format.md; echo '---review'; sed -n '140,170p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/state/state-format.md
- a `{basename(spec_folder)}-pt-NN` subfolder is allocated only when prior content already exists for a non-matching target;
- continuation runs reuse the existing matching packet.

This avoids an unnecessary `pt-01` wrapper on first runs.

**Example (first run on a child phase):** `.../026-graph.../019-system-hardening/001-initial-research/004-desc-regen/` writes to `004-desc-regen/research/` directly.

**Example (subsequent run with prior content for a different target):** `004-desc-regen/research/004-desc-regen-pt-02/` (pt-NN allocated as a sibling to the prior content).

### Frontier Node Store (expansive depth only)

`research/frontier-nodes.jsonl` is the node-status store for a run started with `--depth-mode=expansive`. A default run never creates it. Rows are append-only and carry `{nodeId, parentId, depth, scope, question, status, reason, iteration, dedupeKey}`. The last row for a node wins, so a status change is one more row rather than an edit. `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs` is the only writer.

Statuses are `open`, `active`, `answered`, `blocked`, `exhausted`, `abstained` and `pruned`. A frontier question resolves when its node reaches a terminal status.

The reducer projects the store as `registry.frontier {nodes, stats, budget}`, applies the depth budget so an unspent share rolls down to the next depth only, closes a spent band's open nodes as `exhausted` with reason `budget`, and adds a `## 6B. FRONTIER` dashboard section.

---

## 3. OWNERSHIP MODEL

| Owner | Writes |
|-------|--------|
| Agent iteration | `iterations/iteration-NNN.md`, JSONL iteration/event append, optional progressive synthesis contribution |
| Workflow reducer | strategy machine-owned sections, findings registry, dashboard |
| Workflow synthesis | `research.md`, lifecycle snapshots |
---review
| lineageMode | string | `"new"` | `new`, `resume`, `restart`, `completed-continue` (set by `--extend=N`). The current runtime never emits `fork` |
| generation | number | 1 | Lineage generation number, incremented on `restart`, unchanged on `resume` |
| continuedFromRun | number \| null | `null` | Count of distinct iteration runs in the state log, error and timeout rows included, when `--extend=N` reopened a completed run (`completed-continue`). A new run or a restart writes `null`, and a resume keeps the stored value |
| maxIterations | number | 7 | Hard cap on loop iterations |
| convergenceThreshold | number | 0.10 | Stop when severity-weighted new findings ratio below this |
| stuckThreshold | number | 2 | Consecutive no-progress iterations before recovery |
| severityThreshold | string | `"P2"` | Minimum severity to report: `P0`, `P1`, `P2` |
| crossReference | object | -- | Core (hard-gated) and overlay (advisory) protocol sets |
| qualityGateThreshold | boolean | true | Whether binary quality gates are enforced |
| specFolder | string | -- | Spec folder path (relative to specs/) |
| status | string | `"initialized"` | `initialized`, `running`, `converged`, `stuck`, `complete`, `error` |
| releaseReadinessState | string | `"in-progress"` | `in-progress`, `converged`, `release-blocking` |
| fileProtection | object | -- | Mutability declarations (see protection levels below) |
| reducer | object | -- | Canonical reducer inputs, outputs, and metrics names |
| antiConvergence | object | `{ minIterations: 2, convergenceMode: "default", depthMode: "default", stopPolicy: "fail-closed" }` | Anti-convergence settings: the review iteration floor, the convergence mode and the depth mode |
| expansiveDepth | object | `{ maxDepth: 3, maxChildrenPerNode: 6, budgetSplit: {depth1: 0.5, depth2: 0.35, depth3: 0.15}, maxFrontierNodes }` | Caps for `--depth-mode=expansive`; `maxFrontierNodes` defaults to half of `maxIterations` unless the config sets a number |

### File Protection Levels

| Level | Meaning |
|-------|---------|
| immutable | Cannot be modified after creation (config: the terminal `status: complete` flip is the one permitted write) |
| append-only | New content added at end only |
| mutable | Can be read, edited, overwritten |
| write-once | Created once, never modified |
| auto-generated | System-managed, overwritten each refresh |
| operator-controlled | Created or deleted only by the human/operator to control loop state |

### Review Dimensions

| Dimension | Priority | Required for Coverage |

A fan-out lineage reads only its own partition, so one whose nodes are answered stops rather than running to its cap. .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md:467:Next Focus reads, in precedence order: blocked-stop recovery, the pivot, the active frontier node, the leaf's `nextFocus`, then the first uncovered dimension. Stuck recovery closes the active node `exhausted` with reason `stuck` before recovery continues. .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/protocol/quick-reference.md:48:| `--depth-mode` | default | `expansive` seeds depth-1 sub-target nodes, reviews the active node's next uncovered dimension, vetoes a stop while nodes stay open, and stops on `frontier_drained` once every node is closed. Growth past depth 1 needs `--max-iterations` above twice the live node count |
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/protocol/quick-reference.md:164:**Frontier veto:** Under `--depth-mode=expansive` an open or active frontier node turns a stop candidate into `CONTINUE` with reasonDetail `frontierOpen`. It never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the review floor. A drained frontier (at least one answered node, none open or active) forms the hard candidate `frontier_drained` when no other candidate does; every legal-stop gate still applies.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md:158:`--depth-mode=expansive` is the one stop-path change that grows work instead of ending it. A seed step writes the depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, the active node's next uncovered dimension steers the iteration, and an admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, pruning the proposal on the first failure. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, which no default run creates, and an open node is filed as open search debt, so the run spends the iteration that node still needs. The frontier veto never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the floor above, and stuck recovery closes the active node as `exhausted`. Once every node is closed and at least one was answered, a run that forms no other stop candidate gets `frontier_drained`, which the legal-stop gates still decide, so a run whose frontier work is done stops before its cap. The caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations`. Each node needs at least one iteration, so growth past depth 1 needs `maxIterations` above twice the live node count. At the default budget of 7 an expansive run works its seeds only; raise `--max-iterations`, as the pilots did at 16, to reach depths 2 and 3.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/expansive-depth-mode.md:32:A review stop candidate becomes another iteration while nodes stay open. The `frontierOpen` veto applies to the review stop candidates, including `all_dimensions_clean` and the composite stop, after the minIterations floor. Once every node is closed and at least one was answered, a run that forms no other stop candidate gets `frontier_drained`, which the legal-stop gates still decide.

[0m$ [0mrg -n 'expansive|frontier|depth-mode|maxIterations|budget' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/feature-catalog/feature-catalog.md | head -80
43:### Depth mode flag (--depth-mode)
47:The --depth-mode flag on /deep:research and /deep:review selects default or expansive depth, and leaves the registry-driven workflowMode, runtimeLoopType and backendKind classification unchanged.
51:`mode-registry.json` has no depth field, and the runtime acts only on the expansive value. The behavior is documented in the research and review catalogs.
55:See [`deep-loop-mode-classification/depth-mode-flag.md`](deep-loop-mode-classification/depth-mode-flag.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.
63:The --extend=N flag on /deep:research and /deep:review reopens a completed single-executor run in place, and --extend-minutes=M, valid only with --extend, adds minutes to that run's duration budget. Neither flag changes the registry-driven workflowMode, runtimeLoopType and backendKind classification.
67:`mode-registry.json` has no extension field. The extension sets `maxIterations` to the iterations run so far plus N, keeps the session and generation, and is refused with fan-out. The behavior is documented in the research and review catalogs.
[0m$ [0mrg -n 'expansive|frontier|depth-mode|maxIterations|budget' README.md | head -60
255:Two opt-in flags let a loop reach further. `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a frontier of sub-questions or sub-targets and turns a stop into another pass while one stays open, though never past the iteration cap. `--extend=N` reopens a finished single-executor run in the same session with room for N more passes, and `--extend-minutes=M` adds time. Under the default stop policy it can re-stop at once, so pair it with `--convergence-mode=off` when the run must keep going. A single-executor review can also track custom dimensions beside its four defaults; a fan-out review lineage always reviews the four.
> build · deepseek-v4.1-flash
[0m$ [0msed -n '240,265p' README.md
The commands below are what runs underneath. Type one yourself only when you want a specific workflow or mode.

### Spec Kit Commands

`/speckit:complete` runs the whole path: intake, research, plan, build, verify and save. `/speckit:plan` stops after the plan, so someone can review it before any code is written. `/speckit:implement` builds from an existing plan. Each one accepts `:auto` to run without stopping or `:confirm` to pause at each step.

`/speckit:save` and `/speckit:resume` carry the work across sessions, as [Continuity](#continuity) describes. `/speckit:search` runs the trigger index and the ripgrep recipes over spec docs and skill docs. `/speckit:handoff` hands the work to a fresh session now, instead of waiting for the context limit.

### Deep Research And Deep Review

The deep loops run unattended and stop only when their own evidence says the work is done. `/deep:research` investigates a question one focused pass at a time. Results grow in one research document inside the spec folder. `/deep:review` audits code in passes, ranks every finding P0, P1 or P2 and never edits the code it reviews.

Each pass runs in a fresh context against state kept on disk. A script, not the agent, decides when to stop. A review stays open below its minimum iteration count, while a P0 is active or while a required check still fails, and it ends in a PASS, CONDITIONAL or FAIL verdict. A loop survives crashes and new sessions, because its progress lives in files.

Two opt-in flags let a loop reach further. `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a frontier of sub-questions or sub-targets and turns a stop into another pass while one stays open, though never past the iteration cap. `--extend=N` reopens a finished single-executor run in the same session with room for N more passes, and `--extend-minutes=M` adds time. Under the default stop policy it can re-stop at once, so pair it with `--convergence-mode=off` when the run must keep going. A single-executor review can also track custom dimensions beside its four defaults; a fan-out review lineage always reviews the four.

A loop can also spread across several AI CLIs at once. Each executor runs its own lineage, and for a review the strictest verdict wins the merge. A pool caps how many lineages run at once. A stall watchdog stops a lineage that stops making progress, and a cost cap bounds what each one spends. The [Deep Loop README](.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/README.md) describes both modes and the shared runtime.

### CLI Orchestration

Your main CLI does not have to handle every task itself. The CLI orchestration hub hands a task to another AI CLI on the same machine and reads the result back. The agent you are talking to stays in charge. It writes the brief, waits for the answer and checks it before using it.

This lets you mix subscriptions. You can work in one CLI and send research, reviews or bulk work to another, billed to that CLI's own plan. The bridges are provider agnostic. Each CLI runs whatever providers and models you have set up in it. That is why Skilled supports eight runtimes: whichever plans you already pay for, one runtime is your main CLI and the others receive the work you hand off.

You ask for it in plain words, for example "use cli-opencode to review this diff". The advisor routes the request to the matching bridge. The bridge checks that its CLI is installed and that the dispatch passes its hard rules, then runs it. The deep loops use the same bridges, so one research or review run can spread its passes across several models. [Runtimes And CLI Bridges](#10--runtimes-and-cli-bridges) lists all eight.

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md:141:The lineage block carries `sessionId`, `parentSessionId`, `lineageMode`, `generation`, `continuedFromRun` and `releaseReadinessState`. A first run is mode `new`. `resume` continues the same lineage without changing generation. `restart` opens a new generation and archives the prior packet. `completed-continue` is set by `--extend=N`, which reopens a completed run in the same session and generation and records `continuedFromRun`.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md:158:`--depth-mode=expansive` is the one stop-path change that grows work instead of ending it. A seed step writes the depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, the active node's next uncovered dimension steers the iteration, and an admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, pruning the proposal on the first failure. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, which no default run creates, and an open node is filed as open search debt, so the run spends the iteration that node still needs. The frontier veto never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the floor above, and stuck recovery closes the active node as `exhausted`. Once every node is closed and at least one was answered, a run that forms no other stop candidate gets `frontier_drained`, which the legal-stop gates still decide, so a run whose frontier work is done stops before its cap. The caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations`. Each node needs at least one iteration, so growth past depth 1 needs `maxIterations` above twice the live node count. At the default budget of 7 an expansive run works its seeds only; raise `--max-iterations`, as the pilots did at 16, to reach depths 2 and 3.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md:162:One run can spread the loop across executor lineages. Pass `--executor` once per lineage, or pass an `--executors` matrix. Cap the pool with `--concurrency`. The merged verdict is strongest restriction: any lineage with an active P0, or a required gate still failing at its stop, turns the merged verdict into FAIL.

### `:auto` Setup Resolution

Setup contract: see `.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/references/workflows/auto-mode-contract.md`.

Under `execution_mode = AUTONOMOUS` (from the `:auto` suffix), follow the three-tier flow:

1. **Tier 1 — Resolve confidently** (contract §1): parse `$ARGUMENTS` flags + `PRE-BOUND SETUP ANSWERS:` block (§2) + the Default Resolution Table below (§3). When every required field is resolved, persist to `{artifact_dir}/deep-research-config.json` (shape: `researchTopic`, `specFolder`, `maxIterations`, `convergenceThreshold`, `antiConvergence.convergenceMode`, `antiConvergence.depthMode`, `expansiveDepth.*` (`maxDepth`, `maxChildrenPerNode`, `budgetSplit`, `maxFrontierNodes`), reserved `antiConvergence.divergent: {}`, `executionMode: "auto"`, `resource_map.emit`, `config.executor.*`), bind runtime YAML placeholders, set `STATUS: PASSED`, load `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` with `execution_mode` bound to `auto`. End §0.

2. **Tier 2 — Targeted ask** (contract §1): when 1-2 required fields are genuinely ambiguous AND no default exists, emit ONE narrow question per ambiguous field. Command-specific Tier-2-eligible fields (per the Default Resolution Table below): `spec_folder`. **Ordering rule**: none needed. Missing `research_topic` is absence, not ambiguity — go to Tier 3.

3. **Tier 3 — Fail fast** (contract §4): emit the named-missing-inputs error format with `/deep:research:auto` as the command name. Exit non-zero. Do not load YAML.

`:confirm` path stays unchanged — see the consolidated setup prompt section below.

The run keeps its `sessionId` and generation, records one `run_extended` ledger event, and keeps the prior synthesis as `research/synthesis-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md`. `--extend-minutes=M` adds minutes to the duration budget of the same extension.

The new duration budget is `max(prior, elapsed) + M`, because the clock counts time since `createdAt`, so an extension started after the old budget still runs. Without `--extend-minutes`, `M` is the prior budget, and a run with no duration budget keeps none.

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/README.md:127:`--depth-mode=expansive` lets a run grow its own questions. A seed step writes 3 to 6 depth-1 nodes before iteration 1, and the leaf may propose children only when its prompt carries a frontier block. An admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, and prunes the proposal on the first failure. Next Focus reads the shallowest open node first and writes it as a frontier line, so visiting a node does not read as answering it. While a node stays open the frontier vetoes a soft convergence stop, which never outranks the iteration cap or the other hard stops, and the caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations`. Each node needs at least one iteration, so growth past depth 1 needs `maxIterations` above twice the live node count. At the default budget of 10 an expansive run works its seeds only; raise `--max-iterations`, as the pilots did at 16, to reach depths 2 and 3.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/SKILL.md:91:- `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a depth-capped frontier of follow-up questions beside the seeded key questions. An absent flag means `default`, which keeps the default behavior. The one byte change is the expansive-only persona paragraph in the CLI lineage prompt, which the golden test strips before it compares.

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/SKILL.md:94:- `step_frontier_admit` runs before `step_reduce_state` and prunes a `frontier_proposal` on the first failed check among relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle. .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/SKILL.md:95:- Next Focus reads the shallowest open node first, then the least visited, then the oldest, and writes `Frontier node <id> (depth N, scope S): <question>`.

---

### Review minIterations floor

#### Description

A review run with a configured minIterations floor returns CONTINUE with minIterationsNotReached until its iteration count reaches the floor, so no stop candidate ends a review before the floor. The iteration cap and the halt after three consecutive failed iterations are checked first and can still stop it earlier.

#### How It Works

`convergence.cjs` returns `CONTINUE` with `minIterationsNotReached` below the floor, and the command's fixed stop order checks the floor before the gates. The shipped default is 2.

#### Source Files

See [`loop-lifecycle/min-iterations-floor.md`](loop-lifecycle/min-iterations-floor.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

Audits codebase clarity and the safety of follow-on change.

#### How It Works

Maintainability is the fourth default dimension and covers patterns, documentation quality, clarity, and change cost. It is still required for full coverage even though it carries lower default risk priority than correctness and security.

#### Source Files

See [`review-dimensions/maintainability.md`](review-dimensions/maintainability.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

### Custom review dimensions

#### Description

A review run accepts configured custom dimensions after the four canonical ones, tracks them in dimension coverage, and lets a covered custom dimension reach the all-dimensions-clean stop.

#### How It Works

Custom names are ordered after the four canonical dimensions, judged by their own name and not required for severity coverage.

#### Source Files

See [`review-dimensions/custom-review-dimensions.md`](review-dimensions/custom-review-dimensions.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---
